Use when work touches api/nvidia.com, generated CRDs or clients, validation, DeviceClasses, public configuration, feature-gate stages or dependencies, or upgrade and downgrade behavior.\n---\n\n# DRA API, CRD, and Feature Gate Change\n\n## Purpose\n\nCarry a user-visible or persisted contract change through design alignment, authoritative source edits, generated artifacts, compatibility analysis, documentation, and validation.\n\n## Triggers\n\n- Add or change a CRD, API field, opaque config type, validation rule, DeviceClass, ResourceSlice attribute, Helm value, or CLI flag.\n- Add, remove, graduate, default, compose, or constrain a feature gate.\n- Change kubelet-plugin checkpoint data or migration behavior.\n- Review backward compatibility, Kubernetes resource API versions, or release-note impact.\n\n## Audience\n\n- DRA contributors and maintainers\n- Release owners\n- API and compatibility reviewers\n\n## Inputs\n\n- Proposed behavior and user-facing example\n- Target release and supported Kubernetes versions\n- Affected resource family and binaries\n- Upgrade/downgrade and stored-state expectations\n- Adjacent feature gates and hardware/runtime constraints\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Inspect the worktree and preserve unrelated changes. Record the target branch, `VERSION`, Kubernetes module versions in `go.mod`, and chart version.\n2. Read the proposal process. Require design alignment for any user-facing API, gate, Helm value, ResourceSlice attribute, checkpoint, dependency, or significant behavior change.\n3. Identify each contract and its authority:\n   - Handwritten API types and validation: `api/nvidia.com/resource/v1beta1/`\n   - Feature definitions/defaults/dependencies: `pkg/featuregates/featuregates.go`\n   - Generated deepcopy, clients, listers, informers, and CRDs: generated outputs under `api/`, `pkg/nvidia.com/`, and `deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/crds/`\n   - Helm defaults and rendering: `deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/values.yaml` and `templates/`\n   - Admission validation: `cmd/webhook/`\n   - Kubelet checkpoints and migrations: checkpoint files in both kubelet-plugin command directories\n   - Public reference docs: `site/content/docs/reference/`\n4. Search all readers, writers, defaults, validators, serializers, tests, templates, examples, and docs before editing. Identify the single authoritative state owner.\n5. Define compatibility:\n   - Preserve wire names and semantics unless a reviewed migration exists.\n   - Use optional fields and safe zero values for additive checkpoint changes; document old/new binary behavior.\n   - Account for the Kubernetes DRA API level used by each supported Kubernetes minor.\n   - Treat ResourceSlice attribute names and CEL access paths as public contracts.\n   - State CRD update ordering and Helm's CRD upgrade limitations.\n6. For a feature gate, define stage, default, introduction version, dependencies, mutual exclusions, composition with adjacent gates, graduation evidence, and removal plan. Compare the code registry with the feature-gate docs and report drift.\n7. Implement handwritten sources first. Never hand-edit generated files as the source of truth.\n8. Regenerate with the repository workflow. Review the generated diff for unintended schema, default, required-field, RBAC, or client changes.\n9. Update Helm values/templates, webhook validation, DeviceClasses, examples, API/feature-gate/Helm/ResourceSlice docs, upgrade guidance, and PR release notes as applicable.\n10. Run the change-appropriate validation from `$dra-build-test-codegen-ci`, including focused unit tests, `make generate`, `make check-generate`, `make test`, `make check`, and `make helm-lint` where relevant.\n11. Add upgrade/downgrade and negative tests. Include real-cluster coverage only after explicit approval.\n12. Return a contract-change summary, compatibility matrix, generated-file list, validation evidence, docs/release impact, and open risks.\n\n## Expected Outputs\n\n- API or feature-gate change plan\n- Implementation patch and generated artifacts\n- Compatibility and migration matrix\n- Review findings and release-note text\n\n## References\n\n- Public API reference: `site/content/docs/reference/api.md`\n- Public feature gates: `site/content/docs/reference/feature-gates.md`\n- Public ResourceSlice attributes: `site/content/docs/reference/resourceslice-attributes.md`\n- Public Helm values: `site/content/docs/reference/helm-values.md`\n- Public proposal process: `site/content/contribute/proposals/_index.md`\n- Public Kubernetes DRA concepts: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/\n- Public Kubernetes API reference for ResourceSlice: https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubernetes-api/resource/resource-slice-v1/\n- Internal supplemental: none provided by this skill\n\n## Validation\n\n- Verify handwritten and generated sources agree.\n- Verify API validation accepts supported old objects and rejects new invalid combinations.\n- Verify `make check-generate` leaves no unexplained generated diff.\n- Verify Helm defaults, template validation, binary flags, and feature-gate docs agree with code.\n- Verify upgrade, rollback, in-flight claim, restart, and checkpoint behavior is explicit.\n- For the scenario \"add an optional field to `GpuConfig`\", trace it through Go types, validation, webhook decoding, plugin consumption, generated artifacts, docs, samples, unit tests, and release notes.\n- Report any existing drift encountered; do not normalize it silently outside the requested scope.\n\n## Safety Constraints\n\n- Inspect the current diff before and after generation; never discard unrelated work.\n- Ask before running live-cluster tests or applying CRDs, charts, or workloads.\n- Do not delete or rewrite checkpoint files as a troubleshooting shortcut without an approved recovery plan.\n- Never expose secrets, kubeconfigs, credentials, or customer data.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Do not bypass proposal or maintainer review for public contracts.\n- Do not promise support solely because code compiles.\n- Do not manually edit generated outputs in place of changing their source.\n\n## Owners\n\n- DRA engineering\n- DRA API and release reviewers\n\n## Refresh Policy\n\n- Review every DRA driver release and Kubernetes dependency minor bump.\n- Review after CRD/codegen tool changes, feature-gate lifecycle changes, checkpoint format changes, or GPU Operator compatibility updates.\n- Reconcile code and public reference docs before relying on a gate list or default.\n\n## Registration Notes\n\nKeep this `SKILL.md` as the shared source of truth. They are optional; omit them to use driver defaults. Some fields require feature gates to be enabled.\n\n### GpuConfig\n\nConfigures a full GPU device. \nTarget DeviceClass: `gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n  timeSlicingConfig:\n    interval: Default       # Default | Short | Medium | Long\n```\n\nWith MPS (Multi-Process Service):\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: GpuConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50             # optional, integer\n    defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit: \"4Gi\"         # optional, quantity\n    multiUser: true                               # optional, default false\n    defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit:            # optional, map\n      \"0\": \"2Gi\"\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Sharing strategy and its configuration. Omit to use the device exclusively. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS` (Multi-Process Service). |\n| `sharing.timeSlicingConfig.interval` | string | Time-slice duration: `Default`, `Short`, `Medium`, or `Long`. Requires the [`TimeSlicingSettings`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultActiveThreadPercentage` | integer | Thread percentage limit applied to all processes sharing the GPU. Requires the [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit` | quantity | Pinned memory limit applied to all devices. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.defaultPerDevicePinnedMemoryLimit` | map | Per-device override of `defaultPinnedDeviceMemoryLimit`. Keys are device index (integer) or UUID string. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig.multiUser` | bool | Starts the MPS control daemon in multi-user mode so processes with different user IDs can share the device, defaults to `false` when omitted, and requires a Volta or newer GPU. |\n\n\n### MigDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a MIG device slice. Target DeviceClass: `mig.nvidia.com`.\n\nWith time-slicing:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: TimeSlicing\n```\n\nWith MPS:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: MigDeviceConfig\nsharing:\n  strategy: MPS\n  mpsConfig:\n    defaultActiveThreadPercentage: 50\n    multiUser: true\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `sharing` | object | Optional. Supports `TimeSlicing` and `MPS` strategies. |\n| `sharing.strategy` | string | `TimeSlicing` or `MPS`. |\n| `sharing.mpsConfig` | object | Supports the same MPS fields, defaults, and Volta-or-newer `multiUser` requirement as `GpuConfig.sharing.mpsConfig` and requires [`MPSSupport`](feature-gates.md). |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\n`timeSlicingConfig` is not available for MIG devices. Time-slicing on MIG slices does not support interval configuration.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### VfioDeviceConfig\n\nConfigures a VFIO passthrough device. Target DeviceClass: `vfio.gpu.nvidia.com`.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: VfioDeviceConfig\niommu:\n  backendPolicy: LegacyOnly   # LegacyOnly | PreferIommuFD\n  enableAPIDevice: false       # optional\n```\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `iommu` | object | Optional. IOMMU backend configuration. Omit to use defaults (`LegacyOnly`, API device disabled). |\n| `iommu.backendPolicy` | string | `LegacyOnly` (default) or `PreferIommuFD`. Selects the IOMMU backend used for device passthrough. |\n| `iommu.enableAPIDevice` | bool | Optional. Expose `/dev/iommu` or `/dev/vfio/vfio` to the workload. Defaults to `false`. Requires the [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`PassthroughSupport`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false). `iommu.enableAPIDevice` additionally requires [`DeviceMetadata`](feature-gates.md) (Alpha, default: false).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n## ComputeDomain CRDs\n\nComputeDomains use two Custom Resource Definitions. Unlike the GPU opaque types above, these are concrete Kubernetes resources that exist independently of `ResourceClaim` specs.\n\n### ComputeDomain\n\nA user-created resource that provisions an ephemeral multi-node NVLink fabric. Creating one triggers the controller to deploy a per-domain IMEX daemon fleet and generate `ResourceClaimTemplate` objects that workload pods reference to claim IMEX channels. The spec is immutable after creation.\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: resource.nvidia.com/v1beta1\nkind: ComputeDomain\nmetadata:\n  name: my-compute-domain\nspec:\n  numNodes: 0\n  channel:\n    resourceClaimTemplate:\n      name: my-channel\n    allocationMode: Single        # Single (default) | All\n```\n\n#### Spec fields\n\n| Field | Type | Default | Description |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `spec.numNodes` | integer | — | Deprecated. Set to `0` when using the default [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md) feature gate. Formerly used to gate workload startup until a specific number of IMEX daemons joined. |\n| `spec.channel.resourceClaimTemplate.name` | string | — | Name of the `ResourceClaimTemplate` the controller creates for workload pods to claim a channel from. |\n| `spec.channel.allocationMode` | string | `Single` | `Single` allocates one IMEX channel per claim. `All` allocates the maximum number of channels available in the IMEX domain. |\n\n#### Status fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `status.status` | string | `Ready` when all expected IMEX daemons have joined; `NotReady` otherwise. |\n| `status.nodes[].name` | string | Node name. |\n| `status.nodes[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `status.nodes[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier for the node. |\n| `status.nodes[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Used to map IPs to DNS names. |\n| `status.nodes[].status` | string | Per-node daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. |\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nDo not gate workload startup on `status.status`. The status field is informational. IMEX daemons start as soon as their local node joins without waiting for all peers.\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n\n### ComputeDomainClique\n\nA `ComputeDomainClique` is a driver-managed resource created automatically by the IMEX daemon on each node. You do not create or modify these directly.\n\nEach `ComputeDomainClique` is namespaced to the driver namespace and named as `<computeDomainUID>.<cliqueID>`. It tracks which nodes have joined a specific NVLink clique within a `ComputeDomain` and their daemon readiness.\n\nThe ComputeDomain plugin reads these objects to verify that the local IMEX daemon is ready before allowing a workload container to start.\n\n{{% alert title=\"Note\" %}}\nRequires [`ComputeDomainCliques`](feature-gates.md) (Beta, default: true), which depends on [`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames`](feature-gates.md).\n{{% /alert %}}\n\n#### Fields\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `daemons[].nodeName` | string | Node name. |\n| `daemons[].ipAddress` | string | Node IP used by the IMEX daemon. |\n| `daemons[].cliqueID` | string | NVLink clique identifier. |\n| `daemons[].index` | integer | Deterministic index for the node within its clique. Maps to a DNS name. |\n| `daemons[].status` | string | Daemon readiness: `Ready` or `NotReady`. Defaults to `NotReady`. |\n\n\n## ComputeDomain opaque configuration types\n\nThe following opaque types are set in the `ResourceClaim` specs generated by the ComputeDomain controller. They are managed automatically, you do not set these directly. May be absent when `Single` (the default). |\n\n### ComputeDomainDaemonConfig\n\nOpaque configuration for IMEX daemon `ResourceClaim` objects. Set by the controller in the daemon DaemonSet.\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `domainID` | string | UID of the parent `ComputeDomain` resource. |\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
